is the total number of items recommended by the algorithm by the end of time
T
. Also,
\rho^{Z_{k^{*}}}=k^{*}=I_{\mathsf{total}}^{T}
and
r_{j}^{Z_{k^{*}}}=r_{\textit{j}}^{(T,N)}
for all
\textit{j}\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]
.

Fix item type
j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]
. The sequence
M^{t,u}_{j}=r_{j}^{t,u}-\rho^{t,u}/\texttt{q}_{I}
is a martingale with respect to the filtration
\mathcal{F}^{t,u}=\sigma(r^{1,1},\dots,r^{t,u})

9
9

9

To see that, define the event
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{new}}_{u,t}=\{\text{the item }a_{u,t}\text{ has not been recommended before to anybody}\}
where the order is based on the lexicographic order we define in the proof of Lemma
B.2
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[r_{j}^{t,u}-\tfrac{\rho^{t,u}}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}^{t,u-1}\Big]

\displaystyle-\Big[r_{j}^{t,u-1}-\tfrac{\rho^{t,u-1}}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\,\Big]\overset{(a)}{=}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathds{1}[\tau_{I}(a_{u,t})=j]-\frac{1}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}^{t,u-1},\,\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{new}}_{u,t}\Big]\,\,\mathbb{P}\Big[\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{new}}_{u,t}\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}^{t,u-1}\Big]\overset{(b)}{=}0\,,

(a) uses the fact that
condition on event
\big(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{new}}_{u,t}\big)^{c}
, we have
\rho^{t,u}=\rho^{t,u-1}
and
r_{j}^{t,u}=r_{j}^{t,u-1}
.
Equality (b) uses the assumption in the model which states that the prior distribution of type of an item which has not been recommended before is uniform over
[\texttt{q}_{I}]
. Hence,
\mathbb{P}\Big[\tau_{I}(a_{u,t})=j\,\Big|\,\mathcal{F}^{t,u-1},\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{new}}_{u,t}\Big]=1/\texttt{q}_{I}
.
.
It follows that
\widetilde{M}^{k}_{j}:=M^{(T,N)\wedge Z_{k}}_{j}
is martingale as well, this time with respect to
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{k}:=\mathcal{F}^{(T,N)\wedge Z_{k}}
. Since
Z_{k^{*}}\leq(T,N)
, we have
\widetilde{M}^{k^{*}}_{j}=M^{Z_{k^{*}}}_{j}
. We will use this notation to prove statement of the claim in three different regimes.
First, we would like to apply martingale concentration (Lemma
H.2
) to
\widetilde{M}^{k}_{j}
, and to this end observe that
\mathrm{Var}(\widetilde{M}^{k}_{j}|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{k-1})\leq 1/\texttt{q}_{I}
and
|\widetilde{M}^{k}_{j}-\widetilde{M}^{k-1}|\leq 1
almost surely.

Step 1

For any
k\geq k_{2}:=2\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}
, Lemma
H.2
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\widetilde{M}_{j}^{k}\geq\frac{3k}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\Big]\leq\exp\bigg(\frac{-9k^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I}^{2}}{2(k/\texttt{q}_{I}+k/\texttt{q}_{I})}\bigg)=\exp\Big(-\frac{2k}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\Big)\,.

This gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}

\displaystyle r_{j}^{Z_{k^{*}}}\geq\Theta_{\texttt{q}_{I}}(k^{*}),k^{*}\geq k_{2}\Big]\leq\mathbb{P}\Big[\exists k\geq k_{2}\text{ s.t. }\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r_{j}^{Z_{k}}\geq\tfrac{4k}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{P}\Big[\exists k\geq k_{2}\text{ s.t. }\widetilde{M}_{j}^{k}\geq\tfrac{3k}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\sum_{k\geq k_{2}}\exp\big(-\tfrac{2k}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\big)=\frac{\exp\big(-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\big)}{1-\exp(-2/\texttt{q}_{I})}\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{1}{\texttt{q}_{I}^{2}}\,.

(71)

where (a) uses
\rho^{Z_{k}}=k
. (b) uses a union bound and the inequality in the last display. (c) uses definition of
k_{2}
and
{1-\exp(-2/\texttt{q}_{I})}>\texttt{q}_{I}^{-2}
(which is derived using
e^{-a}\leq 1-a+a^{2}/2
and
\texttt{q}_{I}>1
).

Step 2

For any
k_{2}>k
we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\widetilde{M}_{j}^{k}\geq 6\log\texttt{q}_{I}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(\frac{-36\log^{2}\texttt{q}_{I}}{2(k/\texttt{q}_{I}+2\log\texttt{q}_{I})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(\frac{-36\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{2k_{2}/(\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I})+4}\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{\texttt{q}_{I}^{4}}\,.

This gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r_{j}^{Z_{k^{*}}}\geq\Theta_{\texttt{q}_{I}}(k^{*}),k^{*}<k_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big[\exists k<k_{2}\text{ s.t. }\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r_{j}^{Z_{k}}\geq 8\log\texttt{q}_{I}\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\,\mathbb{P}\Big[\exists k<k_{2}\text{ s.t. }\widetilde{M}_{j}^{k}\geq 6\log\texttt{q}_{I}\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\sum_{k<k_{2}}\frac{1}{\texttt{q}_{I}^{4}}\leq\frac{k_{2}}{\texttt{q}_{I}^{4}}\leq\frac{1}{\texttt{q}_{I}^{2}}\,.

(72)

(a) uses
\rho^{Z_{k}}=k
. (b) uses the inequality in the above display.

Step 3

This step,
k<k_{1}:=\texttt{q}_{I}/2
, corresponds to bounding the number of balls in the fullest bin when the number of balls,
k
, is sublinear in the number of bins,
\texttt{q}_{I}
(since
k=\texttt{q}_{I}^{1-3\delta}
with
\delta>\frac{1}{8\log\texttt{q}_{I}}
). We will show that in this regime, the number of balls in the fullest bin is bounded by
1/\delta
.
For given
k<k_{1}
, define
\delta=\frac{1}{3}\,\frac{\log\texttt{q}_{I}-\log k}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}
(such that
k=\texttt{q}_{I}^{1-3\delta}
). Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r^{Z_{k}}_{j}\geq 1/\delta\Big]\overset{(a)}{\leq}\texttt{q}_{I}\mathbb{P}\Big[r^{Z_{k}}_{1}\geq 1/\delta\Big]\overset{(b)}{\leq}\texttt{q}_{I}{{k}\choose{1/\delta}}\frac{1}{\texttt{q}_{I}^{1/\delta}}\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{5}{\texttt{q}_{I}^{2}}

(73)

(a) is a union bound over
\textit{j}\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]
. (b) uses the fact that
r_{1}^{Z_{k+1}}=r_{1}^{Z_{k}}+1
with probability
1/\texttt{q}_{I}
and
r_{1}^{Z_{k+1}}=r_{1}^{Z_{k}}
with probability
1-1/\texttt{q}_{I}
independently of
r_{1}^{Z_{k}}
. (c) holds for every
\delta>\frac{1}{8\log\texttt{q}_{I}}
( which is due to
k<k_{1}
) using
{k\choose 1/\delta}\leq(ke\delta)^{1/\delta}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r^{T,N}_{j}\geq 3\frac{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}-\log k^{*}},\,k^{*}<k_{1}\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r^{Z_{k^{*}}}_{j}\geq 3\frac{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}-\log k^{*}},\,k^{*}<k_{1}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big[\exists k<k_{1}\text{ s.t. }\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r^{Z_{k}}_{j}\geq 3\frac{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}-\log k}\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}k_{1}\frac{5}{\texttt{q}_{I}^{2}}\leq\frac{5}{\texttt{q}_{I}}

(74)

(a) uses
r^{Z_{k^{*}}}_{j}=r^{(T,N)}_{j}
. (b) uses a union bound and (
73
). Last inequality uses
k_{1}<\texttt{q}_{I}
.

Step 4

This step uses a variation of the Birthday Paradox to bound
\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r^{T,N}_{j}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r^{T,N}_{j}\geq 2,\,k^{*}<k_{0}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r^{Z_{k^{*}}}_{j}\geq 2,\,k^{*}<k_{0}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big[\exists k<k_{0}\text{ s.t. }\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r^{Z_{k}}_{j}\geq 2\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r^{Z_{k_{0}}}_{j}\geq 2\Big]

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}\Big[r^{Z_{k_{0}}}_{j}\leq 1\text{ for all }{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}1-\mathbb{P}\Big[r^{Z_{k}}_{j}\leq 1\text{ for all }{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}\text{ and }k\leq k_{0}\Big]

\displaystyle=1-\prod_{m=1}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{P}\Big[r^{Z_{m}}_{j}\leq 1\text{ for all }{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}\,\big|\,r^{Z_{m-1}}_{j}\leq 1\text{ for all }{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}1-\prod_{m=1}^{k_{0}}\left(1-\frac{m-1}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\right)\leq 1-\big(1-\frac{k_{0}}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\big)^{k_{0}}\overset{(d)}{\leq}\frac{2k_{0}^{2}}{\texttt{q}_{I}}\leq\frac{2}{9}\,.

(75)

(a) and (b) use the fact that
r^{Z_{k}}_{j}
is a nondecreasing function of
k
. We define
r_{j}^{Z_{0}}=0
. The type of the
(k+1)
-th drawn item is independent of the type of the previous
k
drawn items. Hence, conditional on
r^{Z_{k}}=\big(r_{1}^{Z_{k}},\cdots,r_{\texttt{q}_{I}}^{Z_{k}}\big)
, the random variable
r^{Z_{k+1}}
is independent of
r^{Z_{k-1}}
. This gives equality (c).
(d) uses
\exp\big(k\log(1-k/\texttt{q}_{I})\big)\geq\exp\big(-k^{2}/(\texttt{q}_{I}-k)\big)\geq\exp\big(-2k^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I}\big)\geq 1-2k^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I}
for
k\leq k_{0}\leq\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\leq\texttt{q}_{I}/2
.

We put it all together,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r_{j}^{T}\geq\Theta_{\texttt{q}_{I}}(I_{\mathsf{total}}^{T})\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r_{j}^{Z_{k^{*}}}\geq\Theta_{\texttt{q}_{I}}(k^{*})\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r_{j}^{Z_{k^{*}}}\geq\Theta_{\texttt{q}_{I}}(k^{*}),k^{*}\geq k_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r_{j}^{Z_{k^{*}}}\geq\Theta_{\texttt{q}_{I}}(k^{*}),\,k_{0}\leq k^{*}<k_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r_{j}^{Z_{k^{*}}}\geq\Theta_{\texttt{q}_{I}}(k^{*}),\,\leq k^{*}<k_{1}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\Big[\max_{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]}r_{j}^{Z_{k^{*}}}\geq\Theta_{\texttt{q}_{I}}(k^{*}),\,k_{0}\leq k^{*}<k_{0}\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\frac{2}{\texttt{q}_{I}^{2}}+\frac{5}{\texttt{q}_{I}}+\frac{2}{9}\leq\frac{1}{2}\,.

(a) uses the definition of
Z_{k}
and
k^{*}
. (b)
uses (
71
), (
72
), (
74
) and (
75
). Last inequality uses
\texttt{q}_{I}>10
.
∎

B.3
Information-Theoretic Lower Bound

Theorem
4.12
.

Let
\mathsf{s}_{U}
and
\mathsf{s}_{I}
be as defined in Eq. (
25
).
Any recommendation algorithm must incur regret lower bounded as below with numerical constant
c>0

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq

\displaystyle c\max\Big\{N,\min\{NT,N\mathsf{s}_{U},\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\},\min\{\texttt{q}_{U}T,\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\},

\displaystyle\min\big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\mathsf{s}_{U}\big\},\min\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\},\,\min\big\{T\mathsf{s}_{I},T\texttt{q}_{U}\big\}\Big\}\,.

Proof.

Corollary
4.3
states that

\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq\frac{1}{3N}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{bad}(T)\right]-\frac{1}{12N}T\,.

First, we get rid of the term in the above display. In Lemma
4.4
it is proved that
\mathsf{bad}(T)\geq I_{\mathsf{total}}^{T}
with probability one. Also, since users get new recommendations at each time, by time
T
, at least
T
items have been used by any algorithm:
I_{\mathsf{total}}^{T}\geq T
with probability one.
So,

\mathsf{bad}(T)-\frac{T}{4}\geq\frac{3}{4}\mathsf{bad}(T)\,.

Plugging this into above gives

N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq\frac{1}{4}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{bad}(T)\right]\,.

Next, using Corollary
4.11
gives the lower bound

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq\frac{1}{64}\min_{\gamma\geq 1}\max\big\{f_{1}(\gamma),f_{2}(\gamma),f_{3}(\gamma)\big\}\,.

(76)

where

\displaystyle f_{1}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}T\texttt{q}_{I}/\gamma&\text{ if }\gamma\leq T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})\\
\max\{T,\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\}&\text{ if }T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})<\gamma\leq T/2\\
T&\text{ if }T/2<\gamma\end{cases}\quad\quad\quad f_{2}(\gamma)=\max\big\{\texttt{q}_{U}\gamma,N\min\{\mathsf{s}_{U},\gamma\}\big\}

\displaystyle f_{3}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=T\min\big\{\mathsf{s}_{I},\texttt{q}_{U}\big\}\,.

(77)

The remaining proof of the theorem is just based on finding the minimax in Eq. (
76
) in different regimes and does not contain any major insight. We will include these calculations below for the sake of completeness.

Note that the functions
f_{1}(\gamma),f_{2}(\gamma)
and constant function
f_{3}(\gamma)
are parameterized by the parameters of the system
\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I},N
and
T
. So depending on the realization of these parameters, the above minimax takes different forms.
To lower bound the minimax term, we observe that
f_{1}(\gamma)
is a nonincreasing function of
\gamma
;
f_{2}(\gamma)
is a nondecreasing function of
\gamma
; and
f_{3}(\gamma)
is a constant function of
\gamma
(Figure
5
).

Figure 5:
Schematics of functions
f_{1}(\gamma),f_{2}(\gamma)
and
f_{3}(\gamma)
.

Define

M(T):=\min_{\gamma\geq 1}\max\big\{f_{1}(\gamma),f_{2}(\gamma)\big\}\,.

Since
f_{3}(\gamma)
is constant function of
\gamma
,

N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq\frac{1}{64}\max\left\{M(T),\,T\min\big\{\mathsf{s}_{I},\texttt{q}_{U}\big\}\right\}\,.

Computing
M(T)

Define
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
and
\gamma_{3}
to be the intersection of
T\texttt{q}_{I}/\gamma
(the decreasing part of
f_{1}(\gamma)
) and
N\gamma
,
N\mathsf{s}_{U}
, and
\texttt{q}_{U}\gamma
respectively. So,

\gamma_{1}=\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}/N},\quad\gamma_{2}=T\texttt{q}_{I}/(N\mathsf{s}_{U}),\text{ and }\gamma_{3}=\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}/\texttt{q}_{U}}\,.

Note that
f_{1}(\gamma)
is equal to
T\texttt{q}_{I}/\gamma
before the discontinuity at
T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})
.
Also,
f_{2}(\gamma)
is a piecewise linear function of
\gamma
.

We look at three different cases separately. Each case corresponds to a relative position of pieces of
f_{1}(\gamma)
and
f_{2}(\gamma)
.

Case I.
\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}<N\mathsf{s}_{U}
and
8\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}<N\mathsf{s}_{U}

•

M(T)=NT
when
NT/2<\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}
.

•

M(T)=\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}
when
NT/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})<\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\leq NT
.

•

M(T)=NT/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})
when
\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\leq NT/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})<\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}
.

•

M(T)=\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}
when
\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}\leq\min\{NT/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I}),N\mathsf{s}_{U}\}
.

•

M(T)=N\mathsf{s}_{U}
when
\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}<N\mathsf{s}_{U}<\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}
.

•

M(T)=\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}
when
N\mathsf{s}_{U}\leq\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}
.

In This case,

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq C\max\Big\{

\displaystyle N,\min\big\{NT,\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\big\},\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\mathsf{s}_{U}\Big\},

\displaystyle\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}},\,\min\big\{T\mathsf{s}_{I},T\texttt{q}_{U}\big\}\Big\}\,.

(a)

8\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}\leq N\mathsf{s}_{U}

(b)

\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\leq N\mathsf{s}_{U}<8\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}

(c)

N\mathsf{s}_{U}<\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}

Figure 6:
Various regimes of lower bound for regret corresponding to three different cases (a)
8\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}\leq N\mathsf{s}_{U}
, (b)
\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\leq N\mathsf{s}_{U}<8\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}
; and (c)
N\mathsf{s}_{U}<\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}
.

Case II.
\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}<N\mathsf{s}_{U}\leq 8\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}

•

M(T)=NT
when
NT<\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}
.

•

M(T)=\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}
when
NT/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})<\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\leq NT
.

•

M(T)=NT/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})
when
\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\leq NT/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})<N\mathsf{s}_{U}
.

•

M(T)=N\mathsf{s}_{U}
when
\mathsf{s}_{U}\leq T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})<N\mathsf{s}_{U}/\texttt{q}_{U}
.

•

M(T)=\texttt{q}_{U}T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})
when
N\mathsf{s}_{U}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})<\texttt{q}_{U}\gamma_{3}=\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}
.

•

M(T)=\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}
when
\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})

In This case,

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq C\max\Big\{

\displaystyle N,\min\{NT,\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\},\min\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},N\mathsf{s}_{U}\},

\displaystyle\min\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\},\,\min\big\{T\mathsf{s}_{I},T\texttt{q}_{U}\big\}\Big\}\,.

Case III.
N\mathsf{s}_{U}\leq\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}

•

M(T)=NT
when
T/2<\mathsf{s}_{U}
.

•

M(T)=N\mathsf{s}_{U}
when
\mathsf{s}_{U}\leq T/2<N\mathsf{s}_{U}/\texttt{q}_{U}
.

•

M(T)=\texttt{q}_{U}T
when
N\mathsf{s}_{U}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}T/2<\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}
.

•

M(T)=\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}
when
\texttt{q}_{U}T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})<\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}T/2
.

•

M(T)=\texttt{q}_{U}T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})
when
\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})<\texttt{q}_{U}\gamma_{3}=\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}
.

•

M(T)=\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}
when
\gamma_{3}\leq T/(8\log\texttt{q}_{I})
.

In This case,

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq C\max\Big\{

\displaystyle N,\min\{NT,N\mathsf{s}_{U}\},\min\{\texttt{q}_{U}T,\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\},\min\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},

\displaystyle\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\},\,\min\big\{T\mathsf{s}_{I},T\texttt{q}_{U}\big\}\Big\}\,.

Overall, we can combine all three cases to get the following lower bound for regret:

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq C\max\Big\{

\displaystyle N,\min\big\{NT,N\mathsf{s}_{U},\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\big\},\,\min\big\{\texttt{q}_{U}T,\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\big\},

\displaystyle\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\mathsf{s}_{U}\Big\},\,\min\big\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\big\},\,

\displaystyle\min\big\{T\mathsf{s}_{I},T\texttt{q}_{U}\big\}\Big\}\,.

∎

Appendix C
Optimizing Approximate Cost in Heuristic Analysis

In this section, we go over the computations to optimize regret in terms of the parameters of the high level description of the algorithm in Section
5
.
In Eq. (
41
), we derive an approximate bound for regret according to the high level cost-benefit analysis of the sketch of the algorithm.
Plugging in Eq. (
42
) into (
41
) gives a one-dimensional optimization in terms of the algorithm parameter
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
.
We detail this minimization process, though these calculations primarily serve as a demonstration and aren’t critical for understanding the core algorithm.
The significance of this step is showing how the minimization over parameter
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
can result in various bound in different parameter regimes. The choice of optimal
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
will dictate the choice of optimal parameters
{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
and
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
as in Equations. (
38
) and (
40
) in the heuristic algorithm.

The detailed description of the pseudocode of algorithm in Sec.
6
will be analyzed rigorously in Appendix
D
and the parameters in the pesudocode are chosen to minimize the exact bound on regret.

Equation (
41
) as below gives the approximate regret in terms in
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
and
{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}
.

\displaystyle 2N\cdot\mathtt{regret}(T)\;

\displaystyle\approx\;\min\Big\{N\mathsf{r}_{U}+{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{2\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}}{{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}}\Big(T-\frac{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}}{2}\Big)_{+},\,{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}N+\frac{2\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}}{{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}}T\Big\}

\displaystyle:=\min\Big\{\tilde{f}({I}_{\mathrm{rep}}),\tilde{g}({I}_{\mathrm{rep}})\Big\}

Hence, by proper choice of
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}
,

\displaystyle 2N\cdot\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\approx\min\Big\{f(N,T,\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I}),g(N,T,\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I})\Big\},

\displaystyle\text{where }f(N,T,\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I})

\displaystyle:=\min_{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\tilde{f}({I}_{\mathrm{rep}})\quad\text{ and }\quad g(N,T,\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I}):=\min_{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\tilde{g}({I}_{\mathrm{rep}})

We use the approximation
{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\approx\min\{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}/2,\texttt{q}_{I}\}
below to compute
f
and
g
separately:

Computing
f(N,T,\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I})

\displaystyle{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{2\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}}{{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}}\Big(T-\frac{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}}{2}\Big)_{+}={I}_{\mathrm{rep}}\texttt{q}_{U}+2\mathsf{r}_{I}\max\Big\{\frac{2\texttt{q}_{I}}{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}},\,1\Big\}\max\Big\{T-\frac{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}}{2},\,0\Big\}

\displaystyle=\max\Big\{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{4\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}}{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}}T-2\mathsf{r}_{I}\texttt{q}_{I},\,\,{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\texttt{q}_{U}-\mathsf{r}_{I})+2\mathsf{r}_{I}T,\,\,{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}\texttt{q}_{U}\Big\}

To find the minimum of the above display as a function of
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
, note that the first term is a convex function of
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
with minimizer
2\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{\texttt{q}_{U}}T}
. The second term is linear in
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
with slope
\texttt{q}_{U}-\mathsf{r}_{I}
, and the third term is increasing and linear in
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
. Also note that the three terms are equal to each other at
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}=2T
. Also, the first and second terms intersect at
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}=2\texttt{q}_{I}
.

Hence, when
\texttt{q}_{U}>\mathsf{r}_{I}
, the minimizer of the above display is
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}=2\min\{\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{\texttt{q}_{U}}T},\texttt{q}_{I},T\}
and

\displaystyle f(N,T,\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I})=N\mathsf{r}_{U}+

\displaystyle\quad\begin{cases}2\texttt{q}_{U}T,\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}=2T\quad&\text{if }T\leq\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{\texttt{q}_{U}}\\
\vskip 2.0pt\cr 4\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{U}\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}T}-2\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I},\quad\text{ and }\quad{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}=2\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{\texttt{q}_{U}}T}\quad&\text{if }\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{\texttt{q}_{U}}<T\leq\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{\mathsf{r}_{I}}\\
\vskip 2.0pt\cr 2\texttt{q}_{I}(\texttt{q}_{U}-\mathsf{r}_{I})+2\mathsf{r}_{I}T,\quad\text{ and }\quad{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}=2\texttt{q}_{I}\quad&\text{if }\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{\mathsf{r}_{I}}<T\end{cases}

Hence, when
\texttt{q}_{U}\leq\mathsf{r}_{I}
, we have
\min\{\texttt{q}_{I},T\}<\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{\texttt{q}_{U}}T}
and the minimizer of the above display occurs at

\displaystyle{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}=2T\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad\quad f(N,T,\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I})

\displaystyle=N\mathsf{r}_{U}+2\texttt{q}_{U}T\,.

So overall,

\displaystyle 2Nf(N,T,\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I}):=N\mathsf{r}_{U}+

\displaystyle\begin{cases}2\texttt{q}_{U}T,\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}=2T\quad&\text{if }T\leq\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{\texttt{q}_{U}}\text{ or }\texttt{q}_{U}\leq\mathsf{r}_{I}\\
\vskip 2.0pt\cr 4\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{U}\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}T}-2\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I},\quad\text{ and }\quad{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}=2\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{\texttt{q}_{U}}T}\quad&\text{if }\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{\texttt{q}_{U}}<T\leq\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{\mathsf{r}_{I}}\\
\vskip 2.0pt\cr 2\texttt{q}_{I}(\texttt{q}_{U}-\mathsf{r}_{I})+2\mathsf{r}_{I}T,\quad\text{ and }\quad{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}=2\texttt{q}_{I}\quad&\text{if }\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{\mathsf{r}_{I}}<T\text{ and }\texttt{q}_{U}>\mathsf{r}_{I}\end{cases}

Computing
g(N,T,\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I})

\displaystyle{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}N+\frac{2\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}}{{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}}T

\displaystyle={I}_{\mathrm{rep}}N+\max\Big\{\frac{2\texttt{q}_{I}}{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}},\,1\Big\}2\mathsf{r}_{I}T\,.

Notice that the right hand side is the maximum of an increasing linear function of
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
and a convex function of
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
. The minimizer of this term is either the same as the minimizer of the convex term or the intersection of the linear and convex function, whichever occurs first. Hence,

\displaystyle 2Ng(N,T,\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}4\sqrt{N\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}T},\quad\text{ and }\quad{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}=2\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T}\quad\quad&\text{if }T\leq\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{N}{\mathsf{r}_{I}}\\
\vskip 2.0pt\cr 2\texttt{q}_{I}N+2\mathsf{r}_{I}T,\quad\text{ and }\quad{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{*}=2\texttt{q}_{I}\quad\quad&\text{if }T>\texttt{q}_{I}\frac{N}{\mathsf{r}_{I}}\,.\end{cases}

Appendix D
RecommendationSystem
performance analysis

We now carry out the proof of Theorem
6.4
in three steps. First, in Section
D.1
we will define events capturing error in clustering items or users. Then, in Section
D.3
we will categorize the disliked recommendations according to three contributions:

1.

The total number of exploration recommendations made by the algorithm;

2.

The number of bad recommendations in the exploitation phase due to an error in the exploration phase (including potential errors in item clustering or user clustering);

3.

Recommendations made in case the set of exploitable items is too small.

Finally, in Section
D.4
we will bound each of these.

D.1
Error Events

User misclassification

Let the event that each user cluster contains users of only one type be denoted by

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{c}=\big\{\tau_{U}(u)=\tau_{U}(v)\text{ for all }u,v\in\mathcal{P}_{w}\text{ for each }\mathcal{P}_{w}\big\}\,.

(78)

Note that if
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}<\mathsf{r}_{U}
, then the user clustering is the trivial one consisting of one user per cluster and in this case (
78
) automatically holds.

Because user feedback is noiseless and all users of a given type have identical preferences, user clustering errors are one-sided: assuming clustering is carried out at all, users of the same type always end up in the same cluster. Thus,
\mathcal{B}
fully captures the event of an error in user clustering.

Lemma D.1
.

The probability of error in user clustering is
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}]\leq 1/N
.

The proof (in Appendix
E.2
) shows that
\mathsf{r}_{U}
is large enough so that in the case where nontrivial user clustering occurs, then for each pair of distinct user types with high probability there is at least one item in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
that distinguishes them.

Item misclassification

Algorithm
Explore
(Alg.
2
) makes use of sets of items
\mathcal{S}_{\textit{j}}
, defined in Line
5
of
ItemClustering
.
The set
\mathcal{S}_{\textit{j}}
includes the
j
-th representative item in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
and the set of items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
that appear to be of the same type as this representative based on feedback obtained so far.
Let
\mathcal{E}_{i}
be the event of misclassification of item
i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
:

\mathcal{E}_{i}=\big\{\text{there exists }\,\textit{j}\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}:i\in\mathcal{S}_{\textit{j}},\tau_{I}(i)\neq\tau_{I}(\textit{j})\big\}\,.

(79)

For any item
i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
we define
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{c}
to always hold, so
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is defined for all items in the set of items to be recommended in the exploitation phase,
\mathcal{R}_{u}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}\cup\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
.
Recommendations will be made based on the hope that
L_{u,i}=L_{u,{\textit{j}}}
for all
i\in\mathcal{S}_{\textit{j}}
and all
u\in[N]
,
so incorrectly clustering item
i
can result in bad recommendations in
Exploit
(Alg.
6
).

The following lemma is a consequence of choosing
\mathsf{r}_{I}
, the number of positive comparisons needed to declare two items to be similar, to be sufficiently large.

Lemma D.2
.

For any
i
in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
, the probability of item clustering error is

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{i}]\leq{2}/{N}
.

D.2
Sets
\mathcal{R}_{u}
of Exploitable Items

Recall from Line
2
in
Explore
that

\mathcal{R}_{u}=\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}:L_{\mathcal{P}_{w},\textit{j}}=+1}\mathcal{S}_{j}\text{ for each }u\text{ such that }u\in\mathcal{P}_{w}\,,

(80)

where in Line
5
of
ItemClustering
we defined for each
\textit{j}\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}

\mathcal{S}_{\textit{j}}=\{\textit{j}\}\cup\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}:L_{\mathcal{P}_{w},i}=L_{\mathcal{P}_{w},\textit{j}}\text{ for all }w\text{ such that }\mathsf{rated}(i)\cap\mathcal{P}_{w}\neq\varnothing\}\,.

(81)

In words,
\mathcal{R}_{u}
is the union of item clusters with representatives
j\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
such that
u
’s user cluster likes
j
as per
FindPrefs
.

Lemma D.3
.

If users are clustered correctly and item
i\in\mathcal{R}_{u}
was also clustered correctly, then item
i
is liked by user
u
, i.e.

\mathbb{P}(L_{u,i}=-1|i\in\mathcal{R}_{u},\mathcal{E}^{c}_{i},\mathcal{B}^{c})=0\,.

Proof.

On
\mathcal{B}^{c}
any users in the same cluster have the same type and therefore have identical preferences.
On

\mathcal{E}^{c}_{i}
item
i
is clustered correctly only to representatives
j
of the same type as
i
. Next,
\mathcal{R}_{u}
is the union of item clusters liked by users in
u
’s user cluster.
Thus,
i\in\mathcal{R}_{u}
only if a user of the same type as
u
(which could be
u
itself) has liked an item of the same type as
i
(or
i
itself).
∎

Lemma D.4
.

The sets of exploitable items are large enough, i.e., for each
u
we have

\mathbb{P}\big(|\mathcal{R}_{u}|<T\big)\leq{4}/{T}+{2}/{N}\,.

The proof is deferred to Appendix
E.4
, and we now briefly convey the intuition.
First, the set of item types in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
liked by user
u
, called
\mathcal{L}_{u}
, has Binomial size with expectation equal to half the number of item types appearing in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
. Next, when
\mathcal{B}^{c}
holds,
\mathcal{R}_{u}
includes those items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
with types in
\mathcal{L}_{u}
, and hence
\mathcal{R}_{u}
is approximately
\mathsf{Bin}({I}_{\mathrm{rep}}+{I}_{\mathrm{exp}},|\mathcal{L}_{u}|/\texttt{q}_{U})
. Thus, large enough
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
and
{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
guarantee that
\mathcal{R}_{u}
is large with high probability.

D.3
Dissecting the Regret

To bound regret (the expected number of bad recommendations), we partition the set of all bad recommendations made by the algorithm into several categories.
Denote by
T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}
the number of time-steps users spend in
Explore
and recall that
\mathcal{R}_{u}
is the set of items produced by
Explore
to be recommended to user
u
.
Since
\mathcal{R}_{u}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}\cup\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
, the event
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is well-defined for all items in
\mathcal{R}_{u}
and the total regret can then be bounded as

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{u=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}}\mathds{1}\{L_{u,a_{u,t}}=-1\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{u=1}^{N}\sum_{t=T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}+1}^{T}\mathds{1}\{L_{u,a_{u,t}}=-1,a_{u,t}\notin\mathcal{R}_{u}\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{u=1}^{N}\sum_{t=T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}+1}^{T}\mathds{1}\{L_{u,a_{u,t}}=-1,a_{u,t}\in\mathcal{R}_{u},\mathcal{B}\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{u=1}^{N}\sum_{t=T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}+1}^{T}\mathds{1}\{L_{u,a_{u,t}}=-1,a_{u,t}\in\mathcal{R}_{u},\mathcal{E}_{a_{u,t}}\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{u=1}^{N}\sum_{t=T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}+1}^{T}\mathds{1}\{L_{u,a_{u,t}}=-1,a_{u,t}\in\mathcal{R}_{u},\mathcal{E}_{a_{u,t}}^{c},\mathcal{B}^{c}\}\right]

\displaystyle=:\mathtt{A{1}}+\mathtt{A{2}}+\mathtt{A{3}}+\mathtt{A{4}}+\mathtt{A{5}}\,.

(82)

Here
\mathtt{A{1}}
is the regret from the early time-steps up to
T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}
and account for the bad recommendations made by the algorithm during the exploration phase;

\mathtt{A{2}}
is the regret due to not having enough items available for the exploitation phase, which is proved to be small with high probability for a good choice of
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}},{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
and
{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
;

\mathtt{A{3}}
is the regret due to error in user clustering (
\mathcal{B}
is defined in (
78
)), which is small due to Lemma
D.1
;

\mathtt{A{4}}
is the regret due to exploiting the misclassified items (
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is defined in (
79
)), small due to Lemma
D.2
;
finally,
\mathtt{A{5}}
is the regret due to exploiting correctly classified items under the correct user clustering, for which it is intuitively clear and will be verified later that
\mathtt{A{5}}=0
.

D.4
Bounding
\mathtt{A{1}}
-
\mathtt{A{5}}

Bounding
\mathtt{A{1}}

The random variable
T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}
is the number of time-steps the users spend in the exploration phase consisting of
UserClustering
,
FindPrefs
and
ItemClustering
.

In
UserClustering
, all of the
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
are recommended to all users, taking
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
time-steps.
In
FindPrefs
each representative item is recommended to one user per cluster, and user clusters that complete this task are recommended random items until the slowest (smallest cluster) finish. This takes
\lceil{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\min_{w}|\mathcal{P}_{w}|)^{-1}\rceil
time-steps.

ItemClustering
takes
\lceil{\mathsf{r}_{I}{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}}{N}^{-1}\rceil
time-steps to rate items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
by
\mathsf{r}_{I}
users each, since
N
users give feedback per time-step. This is further explained in Remark
6.2
.

Adding these quantities yields

\displaystyle T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}={I}_{\mathrm{usr}}+\Big\lceil\frac{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}}{\min|\mathcal{P}_{w}|}\Big\rceil+\Big\lceil\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}}{N}\Big\rceil

(83)

and

\displaystyle\mathtt{A{1}}\leq N\mathbb{E}\left[T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq N{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{N}{\min|\mathcal{P}_{w}|}\right]\,{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}+\mathsf{r}_{I}{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}+2N\,.

\displaystyle\leq N{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}+\mathsf{r}_{I}{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}+2N+\begin{cases}(2\texttt{q}_{U}+1){I}_{\mathrm{rep}},&\text{ if }{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}\geq\mathsf{r}_{U}\\
N{I}_{\mathrm{rep}},&\text{ if }{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}<\mathsf{r}_{U}\end{cases}

(84)

In the last step we used Lemma
E.2
, which bounds
\mathbb{E}\left[{N}/{\min|\mathcal{P}_{w}|}\right]
.

Bounding
\mathtt{A{2}}

Time-steps
t>T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}
are allocated to
Exploit
. The recommendation
a_{u,t}
to user
u
at time
t
is not an item in
\mathcal{R}_{u}
only if
\mathcal{R}_{u}
has been exhausted (Line
6
of
Exploit
).
At time
T_{0}
, there remain as least
(|\mathcal{R}_{u}|-T_{0})_{+}
unrecommended items in
\mathcal{R}_{u}
, so in the
T-T_{0}
remaining time-steps
a_{u,t}\notin\mathcal{R}_{u}
occurs at most
(T-|\mathcal{R}_{u}|)_{+}
times.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathtt{A{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{u\in[N]}{\sum_{t=T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}+1}^{T}\mathds{1}\{a_{u,t}\notin\mathcal{R}_{u}\}}\bigg]\leq\sum_{u\in[N]}\mathbb{E}\left[(T-|\mathcal{R}_{u}|)_{+}\right]\leq 4N+2T\,,

where the last inequality is a consequence of Lemma
D.4
with the proper choice of parameters
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
and
{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
to be large enough.

Bounding
\mathtt{A{3}}

Term
\mathtt{A{3}}
in (
82
) is the expected number of bad recommendations made in
Exploit
as a result of misclassification of users.
By Lemma
D.1
,
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}]\leq 1/N
, so

\displaystyle\mathtt{A{3}}\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[{\sum_{u=1}^{N}\sum_{t=T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}+1}^{T}\mathds{1}\{\mathcal{B}\}}\bigg]\,{\leq}\,T\,.

(85)

Bounding
\mathtt{A{4}}

Term
\mathtt{A{4}}
in (
82
) is the expected number of bad recommendations made in
Exploit
as a result of misclassifications of items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
.

\displaystyle\mathtt{A{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}{\sum_{u=1}^{N}\sum_{t=T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}+1}^{T}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{R}_{u}}\mathds{1}\{a_{u,t}=i,\mathcal{E}_{i}\}}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\,\mathbb{E}{\sum_{u=1}^{N}\sum_{t=T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}+1}^{T}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}}\mathds{1}\{a_{u,t}=i,\mathcal{E}_{i}\}}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\,N\mathbb{E}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}}\mathds{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{i}\}\overset{(c)}{\leq}N{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}\frac{2}{N}=2{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}\,.

(86)

Here (a) holds since by construction,
\mathcal{R}_{u}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}\cup\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
for all
u
and
\mathcal{E}_{i}
does not occur for
i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
by definition.
(b) holds since
each item
i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
is recommended at most
N
times (at most once to each user); (c) is by Lemma
D.2
, which states that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{i}]\leq 2/N
for
i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
.

Bounding
\mathtt{A{5}}

From Lemma
D.3
we conclude that
\mathtt{A{5}}=0
.

D.5
Upper Bounding Regret

We now combine all the bounds above and plug them into Eq. (
82
) to get

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\leq N{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}+(\mathsf{r}_{I}+2){I}_{\mathrm{exp}}+6N+3T+\begin{cases}(2\texttt{q}_{U}+1){I}_{\mathrm{rep}},&\text{ if }{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}\geq\mathsf{r}_{U}\\
N{I}_{\mathrm{rep}},&\text{ if }{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}<\mathsf{r}_{U}\end{cases}

(87)

The choice of parameters in Section
6.3
gives the statement of Theorem
6.4
.

System parameters
I^{\mathsf{NoItemClust}}

Here
\mathtt{regret}(T)
is increasing in
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
where we require
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}\geq\mathsf{r}_{U}
. Hence
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}^{*}=\mathsf{r}_{U}
.

\displaystyle\mathtt{regret}(T)\leq\mathsf{r}_{U}+6+3\frac{T}{N}+12\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T

(88)

System parameters
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\leq 6N+3T+N\mathsf{r}_{U}\mathds{1}{\{{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}>\mathsf{r}_{U}\}}+(\mathsf{r}_{I}+2)\left\lceil 16\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}}{{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}}T\right\rceil

(89)

\displaystyle+\begin{cases}N\lceil 3{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\rceil,&\text{ if }{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\leq\mathsf{r}_{U}\,,\\
(2\texttt{q}_{U}+1)\lceil 3{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\rceil,&\text{ if }\mathsf{r}_{U}<{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\leq\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}}{3}\,,\\
(2\texttt{q}_{U}+1)\lceil\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})\rceil,&\text{ if }{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}>\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}}{3}\end{cases}

(90)

where we used
\texttt{q}_{I}/3>\mathsf{r}_{U}
.
Note that in this regime,
{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\geq 16\log T
is required.
Also, note that the third and last terms are nondecreasing in
{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}
and the fourth term is nonincreasing in
{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}
.
Optimizing the regret with respect to parameter
{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}
gives the choice of
{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}
in Eq. (
47
) and subsequently regret can be bounded as

\displaystyle\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\leq 6+3\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}+\frac{3}{N}\begin{cases}48\log T+18\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}T}{N}}\quad&\text{ if }\,\,T\leq\mathsf{T}_{Item}\,,\\
\\
24\mathsf{r}_{U}+18\frac{\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}\mathsf{r}_{I}T}}{N}&\text{ if }\mathsf{T}_{Item}<T\leq\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid}\\
\\
2\texttt{q}_{U}\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}+48\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T&\text{ if }\max\{\mathsf{T}_{Item},\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid}\}<T\,.\end{cases}

(91)

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{Item}

\displaystyle:=\max\{T:\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{Item}}=16\log T+2\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T}\leq\mathsf{r}_{U}\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid}

\displaystyle:=\max\{T:\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{Hybrid}}=8\mathsf{r}_{U}+2\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}\texttt{q}_{I}T}{\texttt{q}_{U}}}\leq\texttt{q}_{I}/3\}\,.

The algorithm chooses the system parameters
I^{\mathsf{NoItemClust}}
or
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}
depending on the regime of parameters by comparing the predicted performance of each case in Eq. (
88
) and (
91
) and choosing the better one.

When
T/2<\min\{\mathtt{R}_{I}(T),\mathtt{R}(U)\}
, a trivial algorithm recommending random items to users regardless of the feedback received so far trivially achieves regret
T/2
. Note that in the regime, the
RecommendationSystem
algorithm with either system parameters
I^{\mathsf{NoItemClust}}
or
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}
is exactly doing that.

Sections
6.3.1
and
6.3.2
contain the conditions of optimality for each operating regime.

D.6
Algorithm Guarantee

The regret of algorithm
RecommendationSystem
is bounded in Theorem
6.4
. We give the proof next.

Theorem
6.4
.

Define the parameters
\mathsf{r}_{U}
and
\mathsf{r}_{I}
as in Equation (
44
). Then the following bounds for regret of the
RecommendationSystem
algorithm holds:

\mathtt{regret}(T)\leq\min\Big\{\frac{T}{2},\,\,C\mathtt{R}_{U}(T),C\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\Big\}

for a constant
C
where the functions
\mathtt{R}_{U}(T)
and
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}_{U}(T)=\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T

(92)

and

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\vskip 7.22743pt\log T+\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T},&\text{ if }T<\mathsf{T}_{Item}\\
\vskip 7.22743pt\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}\mathsf{r}_{I}T},&\text{ if }\mathsf{T}_{Item}\leq T<\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid}\\
\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})+\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T,&\text{ if }T_{1}\leq T\,.\end{cases}

(93)

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{Item}

\displaystyle:=\max\{T:\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{Item}}\leq\mathsf{r}_{U}\}\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid}:=\max\{T:\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{Hybrid}}\leq\texttt{q}_{I}/3\}\,.

Figure 7:
The schematics for the functions
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)
and
\mathtt{R}_{U}(T)
.

Appendix E
Proof of lemmas in the performance analysis of the algorithm

The lemmas in this section use the assumptions of the model, the choice of parameters of the algorithm
\mathsf{r}_{I},\mathsf{r}_{U}
(defined in Equations (
44
))
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}},{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
and
{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
in the regimes
I^{\mathsf{NoItemClust}}
and
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}
(Eq. (
45
) and (
46
) respectively). Lemma
E.4
also requires
{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}>16\log T
in the
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}
regime.

We go through the details of the proofs of some lemmas used in analyzing the performance of the proposed algorithm in this section.
We will use the following definition throughout.

Definition E.1

(Number of distinct types)
.

For a set of items
\mathcal{I}
, let
\Phi_{\texttt{I}}(\mathcal{I})
be the number of distinct item types appearing in
\mathcal{I}
. Similarly, for a set of users
\mathcal{U}
, let
\Phi_{\texttt{U}}(\mathcal{U})
be the number of distinct user types appearing in
\mathcal{U}
.

When the set of users or items have independent and uniform types, simple balls and bins arguments in Section
H.2
bound the quantities of
\Phi_{\texttt{I}}(\mathcal{I})
or
\Phi_{\texttt{U}}(\mathcal{U})
.

E.1
Bounding the Exploration Time
T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}

Eq. (
83
) determines the exploration time by

\displaystyle T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}={I}_{\mathrm{usr}}+\Big\lceil\frac{{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}}{\min|\mathcal{P}_{w}|}\Big\rceil+\Big\lceil\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}}{N}\Big\rceil

Eq. (
84
) bounds the term
\mathtt{A{1}}
using the following lemma:

Lemma E.2
.

Suppose that
N>20\texttt{q}_{U}\log^{2}\texttt{q}_{U}
. Then

\mathbb{E}\left[{N}/{\min|\mathcal{P}_{w}|}\right]\leq\begin{cases}(2\texttt{q}_{U}+1),&\text{ if }{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}\geq\mathsf{r}_{U}\\
N,&\text{ if }{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}<\mathsf{r}_{U}\,.\end{cases}

Proof.

When
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}<\mathsf{r}_{U}
, we use the bound
\min_{w}|\mathcal{P}_{w}|\geq 1
.
When
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}\geq\mathsf{r}_{U}
, we upper bound
\min_{w}|\mathcal{P}_{w}|
by the number of users in the smallest user type since all users of the same type always end up in the same cluster. A priori, the type of users are independent uniform on
[\texttt{q}_{U}]
. Hence, an application of Chernoff and union bound gives

\mathbb{P}\big(\min_{w}|\mathcal{P}_{w}|\geq{N}/{2\texttt{q}_{U}}\big)\geq 1-\texttt{q}_{U}\exp(-N/8\texttt{q}_{U})\geq 1-1/N\,.

The last inequality uses
N>100
and
N>20\texttt{q}_{U}\log^{2}\texttt{q}_{U}
to get

\frac{N}{\log N}\geq\frac{20\texttt{q}_{U}\log^{2}\texttt{q}_{U}}{\log\big(20\texttt{q}_{U}\log^{2}\texttt{q}_{U}\big)}\geq 8\texttt{q}_{U}\log\texttt{q}_{U}\,,

Again, the last inequality uses
\texttt{q}_{U}\geq 200
.
Hence,
N\geq 8\texttt{q}_{U}(\log\texttt{q}_{U})(\log N)\geq 8\texttt{q}_{U}\log(N\texttt{q}_{U})
and
\texttt{q}_{U}\exp(-N/8\texttt{q}_{U})\leq 1/N
.

We also bound
\min|\mathcal{P}_{w}|
trivially by
1
to get the statement of the lemma.
∎

E.2
Error in User Classification

In Equation (
78
), we defined
\mathcal{B}^{c}
to be the event that each user cluster contains users of only one type. Since user clustering errors are one-sided and users of the same type always end up in the same cluster,
\mathcal{B}
fully captures the event of an error in user clustering.

Lemma
D.1
.

The probability of error in user clustering is
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}]\leq 1/N
.

Proof.

When
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}<\mathsf{r}_{U}
the clustering over the users is the trivial one with one user per cluster and the event
\mathcal{B}^{c}
holds.
Hence we only bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}]
when
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}\geq\mathsf{r}_{U}
.

We start by bounding
\Phi_{\texttt{I}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}})
, the number of item types appearing in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
.
The types of items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
are i.i.d.
\mathrm{Unif}([\texttt{q}_{I}])
.
The assumptions
\texttt{q}_{U}\leq N
and
\texttt{q}_{I}>100\log N
gives

\mathsf{r}_{U}=\lceil 2\log(N\texttt{q}_{U}^{2})\rceil\leq\texttt{q}_{I}/4
.
Therefore, when
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}\geq\mathsf{r}_{U}
an application of the balls and bins lemma (Lemma
H.3
) gives

\mathbb{P}[\Phi_{\texttt{I}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}})<\mathsf{r}_{U}/2]\leq\exp(-\mathsf{r}_{U}/2)\,.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{w,w^{\prime}}
be the event that distinct user types
w
and
w^{\prime}
are clustered together.
Note that
\mathcal{B}_{w,w^{\prime}}^{c}
occurs precisely when there is at least one item in
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
that is rated differently by these user types.
The elements of the preference matrix
\Xi
are i.i.d.
\mathrm{Unif}(\{-1,+1\})
independent of items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
and their types.
Hence, conditioning on the choice of item types in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
, the probability
of
\mathcal{B}_{w,w^{\prime}}

is
2^{-\Phi_{\texttt{I}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}})}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}_{w,w^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{B}_{w,w^{\prime}}\,|\,\Phi_{\texttt{I}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}})\geq\mathsf{r}_{U}/2\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Phi_{\texttt{I}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}})<\mathsf{r}_{U}/2\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-\mathsf{r}_{U}/2}+\exp(-\mathsf{r}_{U}/2)\leq 2^{-\mathsf{r}_{U}/2+1}\leq 2/(N\texttt{q}_{U}^{2})\,,

where the last inequality uses
\mathsf{r}_{U}=\lceil 2\log(N\texttt{q}_{U}^{2})\rceil
. A union bound over
{\texttt{q}_{U}\choose 2}
distinct pairs of user types gives the statement of lemma. ∎

E.3
Error in Item Classification

The event
\mathcal{E}_{i}
, defined in (
79
) occurs when the item
i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
is misclassified into
\mathcal{S}_{\textit{j}}
for some
\textit{j}\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
such that
\tau_{I}(i)\neq\tau_{I}(\textit{j})
.
To bound
P[\mathcal{E}_{i}]
for any item
i
in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
, define
U_{i}=\mathsf{rated}_{T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}}(i)
for
T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}

where
T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}
(as
defined in Eq. (
83
)) is the time required to finish
Explore
. By time
T_{\small{\textsc{Exp}}}
, items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
are recommended only in Line
5
of
ItemClustering
where the feedback is used to compare them to representatives in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
in Line
5
of
ItemClustering
.

For item
i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
and subset of users
U\subseteq[N]
, define
the potential error event

\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i,U}=\{\text{ there exists }\textit{j}:\tau_{I}(\textit{j})\neq\tau_{I}(i)\text{ and }L_{u,i}=L_{u,j}\,,\text{ for all }u\in U\}

(94)

to be the event that the preferences of users in
U
for item
i
agree with their preferences for items with some item type other than
\tau_{I}(i)
.
Remember that we define the event
\mathcal{B}^{c}
in (
78
) to be the event that the partitioning over the users match the partitioning induced by user types.

The following claim is immediate from the definitions.

Claim 2
.

For any
i
in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
, the error event
\mathcal{E}_{i}
defined in (
79
) happens only when either the potential error event
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i,U_{i}}
defined in (
94
) occurs or there is an error in partitioning the users:

\mathcal{E}_{i}\subseteq\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i,U_{i}}\cup\mathcal{B}\,.

Lemma.

D.2

For any
i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
, the probability of clustering error is

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{i}]\leq{2}/{N}
.

Proof.

Note that in the
I^{\mathsf{NoItemClust}}
regime specified in Section
6.3.1
there is no item clustering and
{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}=0
.
So the lemma is proved for the
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}
regime specified in Section
6.3.2
where
{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}>0
. Note that this regime requires
\texttt{q}_{U}\geq 9\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})
. So we will use this assumption in the proof.

Claim
2
and a union bound gives

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{i}]\leq\mathbb{P}[\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i,U_{i}}]+\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}]\,.

Lemma
D.1
shows that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}]\leq 1/N
.
We bound
\mathbb{P}[\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i,U_{i}}]
next.

Remark
6.2
explains how the items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
are recommended in Line
5
of
ItemClustering
to guarantee that the set of users assigned to each item is uniform at random. Hence, the set of users in
U_{i}
is uniformly distributed over all subsets of size
|U_{i}|=\mathsf{r}_{I}
of
[N]
and the type of each user in
U_{i}
is uniformly independently distributed over
[\texttt{q}_{U}]
.

Since
\texttt{q}_{U}\geq 9\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})
, we have
\mathsf{r}_{I}=\lceil 2\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})\rceil\leq\texttt{q}_{U}/4
.
Since
\mathsf{r}_{I}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}/4
, a balls and bins argument as in Lemma
H.3
upper bounds the number of user types aooearing in
U_{i}
for any
i

\mathbb{P}[\Phi_{\texttt{U}}(U_{i})\leq\mathsf{r}_{I}/2]\leq\exp(-\mathsf{r}_{I}/2)\,.

Since the elements of matrix
\Xi
are independently uniformly distributed on
\{-1,+1\}
and
\Xi
is independent of the user types in
U_{i}
, for any item
j
such that
\tau_{I}(\textit{j})\neq\tau_{I}(i)
we have

\mathbb{P}\big[L_{u,i}=L_{u,j}\text{ for all }u\in U_{i}\,\big|\,\Phi_{\texttt{U}}(U_{i})>s\big]\leq 2^{-s}\,.

Hence, a union bound over
\texttt{q}_{I}-1
other item types and definition of event
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i,U_{i}}
in (
94
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\,\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i,U_{i}}\,\big|\,\Phi_{\texttt{U}}(U_{i})>s\,\big]

\displaystyle\leq(\texttt{q}_{I}-1)2^{-s}\,,

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i,U_{i}}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i,U_{i}}\,\big|\,\Phi_{\texttt{U}}(U_{i})>\mathsf{r}_{I}/2]+\mathbb{P}[\Phi_{\texttt{U}}(U_{i})\leq\mathsf{r}_{I}/2]

\displaystyle\leq(\texttt{q}_{I}-1)2^{-\mathsf{r}_{I}/2}+e^{-\mathsf{r}_{I}/2}\leq\texttt{q}_{I}2^{-\mathsf{r}_{I}/2}\,.

Then, application of Claim
2
, Lemma
D.1
and the above inequality gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{i}]\leq\texttt{q}_{I}2^{-\mathsf{r}_{I}/2}+\frac{1}{N}\leq\frac{2}{N}\,,

where we used
\mathsf{r}_{I}=\lceil 2\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})\rceil
. Note that throughout the paper, all the logarithms are to the base of 2.
∎

E.4
Obtaining Sufficiently Many Exploitable Items

For any
u\in[N]
,
\mathcal{R}_{u}
is the set of items exploitable by user
u
in the algorithm
Exploit
formed in Line
2
of
Explore
.

Lemma E.3
.

For any user
u\in[N],

\mathbb{E}\left[(T-|\mathcal{R}_{u}|)_{+}\right]\leq 4+\frac{2T}{N}\,.

We consider two cases depending on the regime of system parameters
I^{\mathsf{NoItemClust}}
and
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}
.

E.4.1
Proof of Lemma
E.3
with system parameters
I^{\mathsf{NoItemClust}}
.

The parameters are
{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}=0
,
{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}=\mathsf{r}_{U}
, and
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}=6T
as indicated in (
45
).
Here the users are clustered, but structure in item space is ignored.
Hence
\mathcal{R}_{u}
in this regime consists of the items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
which are liked by some user in the same cluster as
u
:

\mathcal{R}_{u}=\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}:L_{\mathcal{P}_{w},i}=+1,u\in\mathcal{P}_{w}\}\,.

To bound
\mathcal{R}_{u}
, roughly speaking, we first argue that at least
\texttt{q}_{I}/3
item types among
[\texttt{q}_{I}]
types are liked by user
u
. Then using
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}=6T
we show that with high probability
T
items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
are among the types liked by user
u
.

Define
\mathcal{T}^{u+}
to be the set of item types liked by user
u
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{u+}

\displaystyle:=\big\{j\in[\texttt{q}_{I}]:\xi_{\tau_{U}(u),j}=+1\big\}\,.

(95)

Since each item type is liked by user
u
independently with probability
1/2
, an application of Chernoff bound in Lemma
H.1
gives

\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{T}^{u+}|<\texttt{q}_{I}/3\big]\leq\exp(-\texttt{q}_{I}/36)\,.

On event
\mathcal{B}^{c}
that users are clustered correctly, the set
\mathcal{R}_{u}
is the set of items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
liked by user
u
.

\text{If }\mathcal{B}^{c}\text{ holds },\quad\quad\mathcal{R}_{u}=\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}:L_{u,i}=+1\}

So

\text{If }\mathcal{B}^{c}\text{ holds and condition on given set }\mathcal{T}^{u+},\quad\quad\mathcal{R}_{u}=\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}:\tau_{I}(i)\in\mathcal{T}^{u+}\}\,.

(96)

The set
\mathcal{T}^{u+}
depends on the preference matrix
\Xi
. The event
\mathcal{B}^{c}
depends on the preference matrix
\Xi
and the type of items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
. Also, the type of items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
are uniformly distributed on
[\texttt{q}_{I}]
and independent of
\Xi
and item types in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
. So Eq. (
96
) implies that conditioning on event
\mathcal{B}^{c}
and set
\mathcal{T}^{u+}
, the random variable
|\mathcal{R}_{u}|
is an independent
\mathsf{Bin}({I}_{\mathrm{rep}},|Lc_{u}|/\texttt{q}_{I})
random variable. Using Chernoff bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[|\mathcal{R}_{u}|<T\Big|\mathcal{T}^{u+},|\mathcal{T}^{u+}|\geq\texttt{q}_{I}/3,\mathcal{B}^{c}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big[\mathsf{Bin}({I}_{\mathrm{rep}},|\mathcal{T}^{u+}|/\texttt{q}_{I})<T\Big|\mathcal{T}^{u+},|\mathcal{T}^{u+}|\geq\texttt{q}_{I}/3,\mathcal{B}^{c},\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big[\mathsf{Bin}({I}_{\mathrm{rep}},1/3)<T\Big]\leq\exp(-T/4)

where we used the choice
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}=6T
in the last line.
Using tower property of expectation over
\mathcal{T}^{u+}
, this gives

\mathbb{P}\Big[|\mathcal{R}_{u}|<T\Big||\mathcal{T}^{u+}|\geq\texttt{q}_{I}/3,\mathcal{B}^{c}\Big]\leq\exp(-T/4)

Now to bound
|\mathcal{R}_{u}|
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{R}_{u}|<T\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big[|\mathcal{R}_{u}|<T\Big||\mathcal{T}^{u+}|\geq\texttt{q}_{I}/3,\mathcal{B}^{c}\Big]+\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{T}^{u+}|<\texttt{q}_{I}/3\big]+\mathbb{P}\big[\mathcal{B}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-T/4)+\exp(-\texttt{q}_{I}/36)+1/N\leq\exp\big(-T/4\big)+{2}/{N}\,,

\displaystyle\text{and }\quad\mathbb{E}\left[(T-|\mathcal{R}_{u}|)_{+}\right]

\displaystyle\leq T\exp(-T/4)+{2T}/{N}\leq 2+{2T}/{N}\,,

where we used
T\exp(-T/4)<2
in the last inequality.

E.4.2
Proof of Lemma
E.3
with system parameters
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}
.

The parameters are as indicated in (
46
):

\displaystyle{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}

\displaystyle=\left\lceil 16\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}}{{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}}T\right\rceil,\,{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}=\mathsf{r}_{U}\mathds{1}{\{{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}>\mathsf{r}_{U}\}},\,{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}=\begin{cases}\lceil 3{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\rceil,&\text{if }{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\leq\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}}{3}\\
\lceil\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})\rceil,&\text{if }{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}>\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}}{3}\end{cases}\,.

Here the items are clustered and the users are sometimes clustered.
To bound
\mathcal{R}_{u}
, roughly speaking, we first argue that there are enough (at least
{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}
) item types in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
liked by user
u
. Then using the assignment of
{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
, we show that with high probability
T
items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
are among the types seen in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
and liked by user
u
.

For given user
u
, let
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}
be those item types learned by the algorithm through recommending
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
which are liked by user
u
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}

\displaystyle:=\big\{\tau_{I}(j):j\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}},L_{u,j}=+1\big\}\,.

(97)

According to Lemma
E.4
, which the choice of
{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
in (
46
)

\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|\leq{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8\big]\leq{2}/{T}+{1}/{N}\,.

The event
\mathcal{B}^{c}
and the set
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}
are functions of the preference matrix
\Xi
, the types of items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
,
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{usr}}
and user types, all of which are independent of
the types of items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
.
Hence, for given fixed set
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}
and condition on events,
\mathcal{B}^{c}
and
|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|>{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8
, the random variable
|\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}:\tau_{I}(i)\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}\}|
is a
\mathsf{Bin}({I}_{\mathrm{exp}},|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|/\texttt{q}_{I})
. Using the choice of
{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}=\lceil 16T\texttt{q}_{I}/{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\rceil
and an application of Chernoff bound gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[

\displaystyle|\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}:\tau_{I}(i)\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}\}|<T\,\Big|\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+},|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|>{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8,\,\,\mathcal{B}^{c}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big[\mathsf{Bin}({I}_{\mathrm{exp}},|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|/\texttt{q}_{I})<T\Big|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+},|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|>{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8,\,\,\mathcal{B}^{c}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big[\mathsf{Bin}({I}_{\mathrm{exp}},\frac{{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8}{\texttt{q}_{I}})<T\big]\leq\exp(-T/4)\,.

(98)

To bound the size of set
\mathcal{R}_{u}
, note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\big[|\mathcal{R}_{u}|<T\big]\leq\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{R}_{u}|<T,\,\,|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|>{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8,\,\,\mathcal{B}^{c}\big]+\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|\leq{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8\big]+\mathbb{P}\big[\mathcal{B}\big]\,,

(99)

where the event
\mathcal{B}^{c}
, defined in (
78
),
holds when each user cluster contains user of the same type.

To bound the first term note that conditioned on
\mathcal{B}^{c}
, the set of items
\mathcal{R}_{u}
contains the items in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
with types in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}
:

\displaystyle\text{If }\mathcal{B}^{c}\text{ holds },\quad\quad\mathcal{R}_{u}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\supseteq}\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}:L_{\mathcal{P}_{w},\textit{j}}=+1,u\in\mathcal{P}_{w},i\in\mathcal{S}_{j}\}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}:L_{u,j}=+1,i\in\mathcal{S}_{j}\}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}:\tau_{I}(j)\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+},i\in\mathcal{S}_{j}\}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\supseteq}\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}:\tau_{I}(i)\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}\}

(100)

where (a) uses the construction of
\mathcal{R}_{u}
in Line
2
of
Explore
and Line
5
of
ItemClustering
.
(b) holds condition on
\mathcal{B}^{c}
, when
u\in\mathcal{P}_{w}
,
L_{\mathcal{P}_{w},\textit{j}}=+1
if and only if
L_{u,j}=+1
.
(c) uses the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}
in (
97
).
Condition on
\mathcal{B}^{c}
, the errors in item partitioning are also one-sided: the item
i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}
is added to
\mathcal{S}_{j}
for any
j\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
if their ratings are the same for a subset of
\mathsf{r}_{I}
random users (see Alg.
5

ItemClustering
). Hence, if
\tau_{I}(i)=\tau_{I}(j)
then
i\in\mathcal{S}_{j}
. Note that there could be misclassified items in
i\in\mathcal{S}_{j}
such that
\tau_{I}(i)\neq\tau_{I}(j)
, hence the set inequality (d) can be strict.

Eq. (
100
) shows that
|\mathcal{R}_{u}|
stochastically dominates
|\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}:\tau_{I}(i)\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}\}|
which gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{R}_{u}|<T,\,\,|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|>{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8,\,\,\mathcal{B}^{c}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big[|\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}:\tau_{I}(i)\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}\}|<T,\,\,|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|>{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8,\,\,\mathcal{B}^{c}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big[|\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exp}}:\tau_{I}(i)\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}\}|<T\,\Big|\,|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|>{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8,\,\,\mathcal{B}^{c}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-T/4)

where the last inequality uses the tower property of expectation on the right hand side of Eq.(
98
)

Plugging in the above display, Lemmas
E.4
and
D.1
in Eq. (
99
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[|\mathcal{R}_{u}|<T\Big]\leq\exp(-T/4)+{2}/{T}+{2}/{N}\,.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(T-|\mathcal{R}_{u}|)_{+}\right]

\displaystyle\leq T\exp(-T/4)+2+2T/N\leq 4+2T/N\,,

where we used
T\exp(-T/4)<2
in the last inequality.

Lemma E.4
.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}
be defined in Equation (
97
).
Under the choice of system parameters in
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}
, Eq. (
46
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|<{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/8\big]\leq{2}/{T}+{1}/{N}\,.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}
be the set of item types in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}
learned by the algorithm after the exploration phase.
Hence,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{\tau_{I}(j):j\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}\big\}\,.

(101)

Note that, with this definition,
|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}|=\Phi_{\texttt{I}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{rep}})
.

Under the choice of system parameters in
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}
,

\displaystyle{I}_{\mathrm{rep}}=\begin{cases}\lceil 3{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\rceil,\quad&\text{ if }{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\leq\texttt{q}_{I}/3\\
\big\lceil\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})\big\rceil,\quad&\text{ if }\texttt{q}_{I}/3<{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\leq\texttt{q}_{I}\,.\end{cases}

(102)

applying balls and bins Lemmas
H.4
and
H.5
to the two above regimes gives

\mathbb{P}[|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}|<{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}]\leq\exp(-{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}})+1/N\,.

Each item type is liked by user
u
with probability
1/2
independently of whether they appear in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}
or not. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|<|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}|/4\,\big|\,|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}|\big]\leq\exp(-|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}|/16)\,.

Overall,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|<{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/4\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big[|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{u+}|<|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}|/4\,\big|\,|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}|\geq{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\big]+\mathbb{P}[|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rep}}|<{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp(-{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}/16)+\frac{1}{N}\leq\frac{2}{T}+\frac{1}{N}\,.

where we used the choice of system parameters in
I^{\mathsf{ItemClust}}
which gives
{\mathsf{k}_{\mathrm{rep}}}\geq 16\log T
.
∎

Appendix F
Optimal Regret – Proof of Theorem
3.1

The statement of the main result in Theorem
3.1
is a looser version of the lower bound on regret in Theorem
4.12
and the upper bound of regret of
RecommendationSystem
algorithm given in Theorem
6.4
.

Subsequantly, to prove Theorem
3.1
, in Section
F.1
, we show that under the modeling assumptions in Figure
2
,
there are universal constants
c
and
C
such that for any time horizon
T
, algorithm
RecommendationSystem
achieves
N\mathtt{regret}(T)\leq C\,N\mathtt{R}(T)\log^{3/2}(N\mathtt{R}(T))
.

This proof is primarily computational and doesn’t offer significant conceptual insights. The reader can skip it without missing crucial information or essential takeaways. We included it for the sake of thoroughness.

In Section
F.2
, we show that if
\texttt{q}_{U}>(\log\texttt{q}_{I})^{2}
and
\texttt{q}_{I}>(\log N)^{5}
then any algorithm must incur
N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq c\,\frac{N\mathtt{R}(T)}{\log(N\mathtt{R}(T))}
.

The function
\mathtt{R}(T)

is defined in a piece-wise manner in the table below, with the support of each piece given in terms of the following functions of system parameters:

\displaystyle T_{1}=\log\texttt{q}_{U},\quad T_{2}=\texttt{q}_{I}/N,\quad T_{3}=\texttt{q}_{I}/\texttt{q}_{U},\quad T_{4}=N(\log\texttt{q}_{U})^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I},\quad T_{5}=\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}\,.

\mathtt{R}(T)

Operating Regime

Range of
T

Conditions For Occurring

T

\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Cold}}

[1,\,\min\{T_{1},T_{2}\}]

1+\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}T}{N}}

\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Item}}

(T_{2},\,T_{4}]

T_{2}\leq T_{1}

\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T

\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{User}}

(T_{1},\,T_{3}]

T_{1}<T_{2}
and
\log\texttt{q}_{I}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}

\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}T}}{N}

\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}

(T_{3},\,T_{5}]

T_{1}<T_{2}
and
\log\texttt{q}_{I}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}

(T_{4},\,T_{5}]

T_{2}\leq T_{1}
and
\log\texttt{q}_{I}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}

\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}+\frac{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{N}T

(T_{5},\,\infty)

\log\texttt{q}_{I}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}

\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T

\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Asymptotic}}

(T_{1},\,\infty)

T_{1}<T_{2}
and
\texttt{q}_{U}<\log\texttt{q}_{I}

\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T

(T_{4},\,\infty)

T_{2}\leq T_{1}
and
\texttt{q}_{U}<\log\texttt{q}_{I}

For the sake of simplicity, we will ignore all universal constant factors and denote them by
c
or
C
in this proof, although these constants may take different values in differet steps.

F.1
Approximate Upper Bound on Regret

Let
\mathtt{R}_{U}(T)
and
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)
defined in Eq. (
49
) and (
50
).
Theorem
6.4
states that algorithm
RecommendationSystem
achieves regret

\mathtt{regret}(T)\leq c\min\Big\{\frac{T}{2},\mathtt{R}_{U}(T),\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\Big\}\,.

Next, we will show that in all regimes of parameterx
\texttt{q}_{I},\texttt{q}_{U},N
and
T

\displaystyle\min\Big\{\frac{T}{2},\mathtt{R}_{U}(T),\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\Big\}\leq c\,\mathtt{R}(T)\log^{3/2}(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))\,,

(103)

to get

\mathtt{regret}(T)\leq C\mathtt{R}(T)\log^{3/2}(N\,\,\mathtt{R}(T))\,.

Throughout, we will use the
2\log(N\texttt{q}_{U})<\mathsf{r}_{U}<4\log(N\texttt{q}_{U})
and
2\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})<\mathsf{r}_{I}<3\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})
. We will also use the assumptions in Figure
2
including
N>100
.

Cold start
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Cold}}
:

In this regime,
\mathtt{R}(T)=T
and

\displaystyle\frac{T}{2}<\frac{1}{2}\,\mathtt{R}(T)\log(N\mathtt{R}(T))\,.

which gives (
103
) for
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Cold}}
.

Item-Item Regime
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Item}}

For
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Item}}=(T_{2},T_{4}]
we have

\mathtt{R}(T)=1+\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}T}{N}}\,.

In this regime, we show that
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)<c\,\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))
whish shows (
103
). Note that
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)
takes three different forms.

Case I:

T\in(T_{2},T_{4}]\cap(1,\mathsf{T}_{Item})

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)

\displaystyle=\log T+\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T}\leq\log T+\sqrt{\frac{3\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})}{N}T}

\displaystyle<2\Big[1+\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}T/N}\Big]\log(NT\texttt{q}_{I})<\frac{1}{4}\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))\,.

where we used
\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))\geq 1/2\log(N\texttt{q}_{I}T)
.

Case II:

T\in(T_{2},T_{4}]\cap[\mathsf{T}_{Item},\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid})
.
In this case,

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}\mathsf{r}_{I}T}

For
T\leq T_{4}=N\frac{(\log\texttt{q}_{U})^{2}}{\texttt{q}_{I}}

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}\mathsf{r}_{I}T}\leq 4\log(N\texttt{q}_{U})+\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{U}\mathsf{r}_{I}/N}\leq 8\log(N\texttt{q}_{U})\sqrt{\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})}\,.

Using
T>\mathsf{T}_{Item}>N\mathsf{r}_{U}^{2}/(4\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}):

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))>\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}T}{4N}}\log(NT\texttt{q}_{I})

\displaystyle>\frac{\mathsf{r}_{U}}{4\sqrt{\mathsf{r}_{I}}}\log(NT\texttt{q}_{I})>\frac{1}{8}\log(N\texttt{q}_{U})\sqrt{\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})}.

where we used
\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))\geq c\log(N\texttt{q}_{I}T)
.

The above two displays show (
103
) for
T\in(T_{2},T_{4}]\cap[\mathsf{T}_{Item},\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid})
.

Case III:

T\in(T_{2},T_{4}]\cap[\max\{\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid},\mathsf{T}_{Item}\},\infty)

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})+\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T

\displaystyle\text{Since }T>\mathsf{T}_{Item}>N\mathsf{r}_{U}^{2}/(4\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I})\text{ (as above)}:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}T}{N}}\log(NT\texttt{q}_{I})

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{8}\log(N\texttt{q}_{U})\sqrt{\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})}>\frac{1}{10}\mathsf{r}_{U}\,.

\displaystyle\text{Using }T>\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid}>\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}{36\mathsf{r}_{I}}:\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}T}{N}}\log(NT\texttt{q}_{I})

\displaystyle>\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}}{6}\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})>{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}{6N}}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})

Finally,
T\leq T_{4}=N(\log\texttt{q}_{U})^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I}<N\texttt{q}_{I}
(since
\log\texttt{q}_{U}<\log N<\texttt{q}_{I}
). So,

\quad\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T<\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}T}{N}}\log(NT\texttt{q}_{I})\,.

The above three displays show (
103
) for
T\in(T_{2},T_{4}]\cap[\max\{\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid},\mathsf{T}_{Item}\},\infty)
.

User-User regime
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{User}}

For
T\in(T_{1},T_{3}]
we have

\mathtt{R}(T)=\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T\,.

In this case, to get (
103
), we will show that
\mathtt{R}_{U}(T)=\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T\leq C\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))
.

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}_{U}(T)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T<4\log(N\texttt{q}_{U})+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T

\displaystyle<4\Big(\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T\Big)\log(N)<4\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))\,.

Hybrid regime
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}
:

For
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}
we have

\mathtt{R}(T)=\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}T}}{N}\,.

In this case, to get (
103
), we will show that
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\leq C\mathtt{R}(T)\log^{3/2}(NR(T))

and will use

\log^{3/2}(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))\geq\log^{3/2}(N+\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}T})>\frac{1}{4}\max\big\{\log^{3/2}N,\log^{3/2}\texttt{q}_{I}\big\}\,.

Note that
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)
takes three different forms.

Case I:
(
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}\cap(1,\mathsf{T}_{Item})
)
When
T<\mathsf{T}_{Item}
,

\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\leq C\mathsf{r}_{U}<4C\log(N)<c\,\mathtt{R}(T)\log^{3/2}(NR(T))\,.

Case II:
(
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}\cap[\mathsf{T}_{Item},\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid})
)
In this regime,

\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)=\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}\mathsf{r}_{I}T}\,.

As above,
\mathsf{r}_{U}<C\mathtt{R}(T)\log^{3/2}(NR(T))
.
Also,

\sqrt{\mathsf{r}_{I}}<C\sqrt{\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})}<C^{\prime}\max\{\log(N),\log(\texttt{q}_{I})\}\,.

So,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}\mathsf{r}_{I}T}

\displaystyle=C\frac{\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}T}}{N}\max\{\log(N),\log(\texttt{q}_{I})\}<C\frac{\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}T}}{N}\log^{3/2}(N\mathtt{R}(T))\,.

Case III:

T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}\cap[\max\{\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid},\mathsf{T}_{Item}\},\infty)

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})+\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T

As above,
\mathsf{r}_{U}<C\mathtt{R}(T)\log^{3/2}(NR(T))
.

For
T>\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid}
, we have
\mathsf{r}_{U}+\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}\texttt{q}_{I}T}{\texttt{q}_{U}}}>\texttt{q}_{I}/24
. Using
\texttt{q}_{I}>100\log N>30\mathsf{r}_{U}
we get
\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}T/\texttt{q}_{U}}>c\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}}{\sqrt{\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})}}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}(T)\log^{3/2}(NR(T))

\displaystyle>C\frac{\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}T}}{N}\max\{\log^{3/2}N,\log^{3/2}\texttt{q}_{I}\}

\displaystyle>C\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}\texttt{q}_{I}}{N\sqrt{\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})}}\max\{\log^{3/2}N,\log^{3/2}\texttt{q}_{I}\}

\displaystyle>C\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})

Finally,
T\leq T_{5}=\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}
. So,

\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}\sqrt{T}<\frac{\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}}{N}\mathsf{r}_{I}<\frac{\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}}{N}\max\{\log N,\log\texttt{q}_{I}\}

Hence,

\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T<C\frac{\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}T}}{N}\max\{\log(N),\log(\texttt{q}_{I})\}<C\,\mathtt{R}(T)\log^{3/2}(NR(T))\,.

Putting it all together gives (
103
) for
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}\cap[\max\{\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid},\mathsf{T}_{Item}\},\infty)
.

Asymptotic regime
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Asymptotic}}
and
\log\texttt{q}_{I}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}

In this regime, we have

\mathtt{R}(T)=\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}+\frac{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{N}T

We will show that
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\leq C\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))
and use

\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))>\max\{\log N,\log(\texttt{q}_{U}\texttt{q}_{I}),\log T\}.

Note that
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)
takes three different forms.

Case I:
(
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Asymptotic}}\cap(1,\mathsf{T}_{Item})
)
When
T<\mathsf{T}_{Item}
,

\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\leq\mathsf{r}_{U}<8\log N<8\,\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))\,.

Case II:
(
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}\cap[\mathsf{T}_{Item},\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid})
)
In this regime,

\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)=\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}\mathsf{r}_{I}T}\,.

As above,
\mathsf{r}_{U}<8\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))
.
Also,

\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}\mathsf{r}_{I}T}<\frac{1}{2N}(\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}+T\mathsf{r}_{I})<\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}{2N}+2\frac{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{N}T\log(N)

Hence,
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\leq 8\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))
for
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}\cap[\mathsf{T}_{Item},\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid})
.

Case III:

T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Asymptotic}}\cap[\max\{\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid},\mathsf{T}_{Item}\},\infty)

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{r}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})+\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T

Note that

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}_{U}

\displaystyle<8\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))

\displaystyle\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}\max\{\log N,\log\texttt{q}_{I}\}<\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{r}_{I}}{N}T

\displaystyle<3\frac{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{N}T\log(N)<3\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))

Hence,
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\leq 8\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))
for
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Asymptotic}}\cap[\max\{\mathsf{T}_{Hybrid},\mathsf{T}_{Item}\},\infty)
.

Asymptotic regime
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Asymptotic}}
and
\log\texttt{q}_{I}>\texttt{q}_{U}
:

In this regime, we have

\mathtt{R}(T)=\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T

Using the proof in the User-User regime,
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{User}}
, we get
\mathtt{R}_{U}(T)\leq C\mathtt{R}(T)\log(NR(T))
.

F.2
Approximate Lower Bound on Regret

Theorem
4.12
states that any recommendation system must incur the regret lower bounded as follows.

\displaystyle\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq c\max\Big\{

\displaystyle N,\min\{NT,N\log\texttt{q}_{U},\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\},\min\{\texttt{q}_{U}T,\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\},

\displaystyle\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\Big\},\min\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\},\,

\displaystyle\min\big\{T\log\texttt{q}_{I},T\texttt{q}_{U}\big\}\Big\}

In this section, we will show that in all regimes of parameterx
\texttt{q}_{I},\texttt{q}_{U},N
and
T

\displaystyle\frac{N\mathtt{R}(T)}{\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))}\leq c\max\Big\{

\displaystyle N,\min\{NT,N\log\texttt{q}_{U},\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\},\min\{\texttt{q}_{U}T,\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\},

\displaystyle\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\Big\},\min\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\},\,

\displaystyle\min\big\{T\log\texttt{q}_{I},T\texttt{q}_{U}\big\}\Big\}\,.

(104)

Note that to show this, it suffices to prove that the LHS is upper bounded by one or two terms in the max.
This proves that for any recommendation system,

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq c\frac{N\mathtt{R}(T)}{\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))}\,.

(105)

To prove Eq. (
F.2
), we will also use the assumptions in Figure
2
.
Additionally, we will use use the assumption
\texttt{q}_{U}>(\log\texttt{q}_{I})^{2}
and
\texttt{q}_{I}>(\log N)^{5}
and definitions of
\mathsf{s}_{U}
and
\mathsf{s}_{I}
to get
c\log\texttt{q}_{U}<\mathsf{s}_{U}\leq C\log\texttt{q}_{U}
and and
c\log\texttt{q}_{I}<\mathsf{r}_{I}<C\log\texttt{q}_{I}
.
For the sake of simplicity, we will ignore all universal constant factors denoting them by
c
.

We go over each regime in the statement of Theorem
3.1
separately to show Eq. (
F.2
) for various forms that
\mathtt{R}(T)
can take.

Cold start
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Cold}}
:

When
T\in[1,\min\{T_{1},T_{2}\}]
with
T_{1}=\log\texttt{q}_{U}
and
T_{2}=\texttt{q}_{I}/N
we have
\mathtt{R}(T)=T
.

For
T<\min\{\log\texttt{q}_{U},\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}/N\}
we have

\min\{NT,N\log\texttt{q}_{U},\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\}=NT=N\mathtt{R}(T)\,.

If
T_{1}=\log\texttt{q}_{U}\leq\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}/N<\texttt{q}_{I}/N=T_{2}
, we are done with proving (
F.2
) for the cold start regime.

Otherwise, if
\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}/N\leq T<\min\{\log\texttt{q}_{U},\texttt{q}_{I}/N\}
, we have
\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))\geq 1/2\log\texttt{q}_{I}
and

\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\Big\}=\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}\geq\frac{N\mathtt{R}(T)}{2\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))}\,.

Item-Item Regime
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Item}}
:

For
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Item}}=(T_{2},T_{4}]
we have

\mathtt{R}(T)=1+\sqrt{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}T}{N}}\,.

In this regime,

\displaystyle\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}

\displaystyle>\frac{\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}\qquad\text{ since }\quad T>T_{2}=\texttt{q}_{I}/N\text{ and, }

\displaystyle N\log\texttt{q}_{U}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}\qquad\text{ since }\quad T\leq T_{4}=N(\log\texttt{q}_{U})^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I}\,.

Using these give

\displaystyle\max\Big\{N,

\displaystyle\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\Big\}\Big\}\geq\max\Big\{N,\frac{\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}\Big\}

\displaystyle>\frac{N+\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}}{2\log\texttt{q}_{I}}>\frac{N\mathtt{R}(T)}{2\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))}\,.

which proves (
F.2
) for
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Item}}
.

User-User regime
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{User}}
:

When
T_{1}<T_{2},
for
T\in(T_{1},T_{3}]
we have

\mathtt{R}(T)=\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}}{N}T\,.

In this regime, since
T<T_{3}=\texttt{q}_{I}/\texttt{q}_{U}
, we have
\texttt{q}_{U}T/\log\texttt{q}_{I}<\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}
.

\displaystyle\max\Big\{

\displaystyle\min\{\texttt{q}_{U}T,\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\}\,,\min\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\max\Big\{\min\{\texttt{q}_{U}T,\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\},\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}\Big\}>\begin{cases}\texttt{q}_{U}T,\quad\text{ if }T<\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}/\texttt{q}_{U}\\
\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}\quad\text{ if }T\geq\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}/\texttt{q}_{U}\end{cases}

\displaystyle>\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log(\texttt{q}_{U}T)}>\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log(N\mathtt{R}(T))}\,.

where we used
\log(\texttt{q}_{U}T)<c\log\texttt{q}_{I}
for
T\geq\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}/\texttt{q}_{U}
.
Also,
