\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\geq cN=c\frac{N\log\texttt{q}_{U}}{\log\texttt{q}_{U}}>c\frac{N\log\texttt{q}_{U}}{\log(N\mathtt{R}(T))}\,.

The above two displays together gives (
105
) for
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{User}}
.

Hybrid regime
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}
:

For
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}
we have

N\mathtt{R}(T)=N\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\,.

This regime occurs only when
\log\texttt{q}_{I}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}
.

\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}=(T_{3},T_{5}]
with
T_{3}=\texttt{q}_{I}/\texttt{q}_{U}
and
T_{5}=\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}
when
T_{1}=\log\texttt{q}_{U}<\texttt{q}_{I}/N=T_{2}
.

\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}=(T_{4},T_{5}]
with
T_{4}=N(\log\texttt{q}_{U})^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I}
when
T_{1}\geq T_{2}
.

Note that

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\geq cN\geq c\frac{N\log\texttt{q}_{U}}{\log\texttt{q}_{U}}>c/2\frac{N\log\texttt{q}_{U}}{\log(N\mathtt{R}(T))}\,.

Now, we have to show
N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq c\frac{\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}}{\log(N\mathtt{R}(T))}
to get Eq. (
105
).

We will focus on three different regimes of horizon
T
:

•

Let
T>T_{3}:=\texttt{q}_{I}/\texttt{q}_{U}
then
\texttt{q}_{U}T>\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}
. Hence, using Eq. (
F.2
),

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\geq c\min\Big\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{U}T}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\Big\}>c\frac{\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}>c/2\frac{\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}}{\log(N\mathtt{R}(T))}\,.

•

Let
\max\{\log\texttt{q}_{U}\log\texttt{q}_{I},N(\log\texttt{q}_{U})^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I}\}<T\leq\texttt{q}_{I}/\texttt{q}_{U}
. The bounds on the interval guarantee that
\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\Big\}=N\log\texttt{q}_{U}
and
\texttt{q}_{I}\geq\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}
.

This interval has overlap with
\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}
only when
T_{1}\geq T_{2}
, hence
N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\geq\texttt{q}_{I}
. Using Eq. (
F.2
),

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\geq c\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\Big\}

\displaystyle=cN\log\texttt{q}_{U}\geq c\texttt{q}_{I}\geq c\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}\,.

•

Let
T_{4}=N(\log\texttt{q}_{U})^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I}<T\leq\min\{\log\texttt{q}_{U}\log\texttt{q}_{I},\texttt{q}_{I}/\texttt{q}_{U}\}
.
This interval has overlap with
\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}
only when
T_{1}\geq T_{2}
, i.e.,
N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\geq\texttt{q}_{I}
and consequently
N(\log\texttt{q}_{U})^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I}\geq\texttt{q}_{I}/N
.

Also,

\displaystyle\texttt{q}_{I}/N\leq N(\log\texttt{q}_{U})^{2}/\texttt{q}_{I}<T\text{ implies }\begin{cases}\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}<N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\\
\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}<NT\end{cases}

Hence,

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\geq c\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq c\frac{\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N}}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}>c\frac{\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}\geq c/2\frac{\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}}}{\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))}\,.

Asymptotic regime
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Asymptotic}}
and
\log\texttt{q}_{I}\leq\texttt{q}_{U}
:

For
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}=(T_{5},\infty)
for
T_{5}=\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}
, we have

N\mathtt{R}(T)=N\log\texttt{q}_{U}+\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}+T\log\texttt{q}_{I}\,.

Using Eq. (
F.2
),

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\geq c\max\Big\{N,\min\{T\texttt{q}_{U},T\log\texttt{q}_{I}\}\Big\}=c/2(T\log\texttt{q}_{I}+N)\,.

Since
T>T_{5}=\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}
then
T\log\texttt{q}_{I}>T>\texttt{q}_{U}\texttt{q}_{I}
.
Also
\log\texttt{q}_{U}<\log N<\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))
. This all gives

CN\mathtt{regret}(T)>T\log\texttt{q}_{I}+N>c\frac{T\log\texttt{q}_{I}+\texttt{q}_{I}\texttt{q}_{U}+N\log\texttt{q}_{U}}{\log\texttt{q}_{U}}>c\frac{N\,\mathtt{R}(T)}{\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))}\,.

Asymptotic regime
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Asymptotic}}
and
\log\texttt{q}_{I}>\texttt{q}_{U}
:

For
T\in\mathtt{T}_{\mathsf{Hybrid}}
, we have

N\mathtt{R}(T)=N\log\texttt{q}_{U}+T\texttt{q}_{U}\,.

Using Eq. (
F.2
),

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)

\displaystyle\geq c\max\Big\{N,\min\{T\texttt{q}_{U},T\log\texttt{q}_{I}\}\Big\}=c/2(T\texttt{q}_{U}+N)\,.

Also
\log\texttt{q}_{U}<\log N<\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))
. This all gives

CN\mathtt{regret}(T)>T\texttt{q}_{U}+N>\frac{T\texttt{q}_{U}+N\log\texttt{q}_{U}}{\log\texttt{q}_{U}}>\frac{N\,\mathtt{R}(T)}{\log(N\,\mathtt{R}(T))}\,.

Appendix G
Bounding Cold Start Time – proof of Corollary
3.2

Corollary
3.2
.

Under the modeling assumptions in Figure
2
,
and assuming
\texttt{q}_{U}>(\log\texttt{q}_{I})^{2}
and
\texttt{q}_{I}>(\log N)^{5}
, there are universal constants
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}>0
such that the following hold:

The cold start time of
RecommendationSystem
is upper bounded as

\displaystyle\textsf{coldstart}(\gamma)\leq\min\Big\{\log^{2}N,\max\big\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{N}\log^{2}(N\texttt{q}_{I}),\,16\big\}\Big\}\,.

(106)

Also, the cold start time of any algorithm is lower bounded as

\displaystyle\textsf{coldstart}(\gamma^{\prime})\geq\min\Big\{\log N\,\log\texttt{q}_{U},\max\big\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\log^{2}\texttt{q}_{I}}{N},16\big\}\Big\}\,.

(107)

Proving this Corollary is primarily computational and doesn’t offer significant conceptual insights. The reader can skip it without missing crucial information or essential takeaways. We included it for the sake of thoroughness.

G.1
Upper Bound on Cold Start

In this section, we prove the upper bound on the cold start time. Using the definition of cold start time in Eq. (
2
), to get the upper bound in cold start time in Eq. (
106
), we will show that there exists
\gamma>0
such that if there exists some
T
such that

\displaystyle T>\min\Big\{\log^{2}N,\max\big\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{N}\log^{2}(N\texttt{q}_{I}),16\big\}\Big\}

(108)

then

\displaystyle\mathtt{regret}(T)\leq\gamma\frac{T}{\log(NT)}

(109)

Note that according to Theorem
6.4
,
RecommendationSystem
algorithm achieves

\mathtt{regret}(T)\leq\min\Big\{\frac{T}{2},\,\,C\mathtt{R}_{U}(T),C\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\Big\}\,,

for
\mathtt{R}_{U}(T)
and
\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)
defined in Eq. (
49
) and (
50
).
So it suffices to show that if there exists some
T
lower bounded as in (
108
), then

\min\Big\{NT,N\mathtt{R}_{U}(T),N\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)\Big\}\leq\gamma\frac{NT}{\log(NT)}

Throughout, we will use the
2\log(N\texttt{q}_{U})<\mathsf{r}_{U}<4\log(N\texttt{q}_{U})
and
2\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})<\mathsf{r}_{I}<3\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})
. We will also use the assumptions in Figure
2
including
N>100
10
10

10

Throughout, we will use the property that if
x>2A\log A
then
x/\log x>A
and if
x<A\log A
then
x/\log x<A
.
.

Step 1

We consider some
T
such that
\log^{2}N<T<N
. Then we show
N\mathtt{R}_{U}(T)<\gamma\frac{NT}{\log NT}
to get (
109
)
11
11

11

Such
T
exists since
N>100
.
.

Since
T>\log^{2}N
then

NT>N\log^{2}N>1/2(N\log N)\log(N\log N)

and

\frac{NT}{\log(NT)}>(N/4)\log N>\frac{1}{32}N\mathsf{r}_{U}\,.

Also, since
T<N
and
N>20\texttt{q}_{U}\log^{2}\texttt{q}_{U}

then
N/\log N\geq\texttt{q}_{U}
and hence

\frac{NT}{\log(NT)}>\frac{NT}{2\log N}>\frac{1}{2}\texttt{q}_{U}T\,.

so fo any
\log^{2}N<T<N
,

\mathtt{regret}(T)<c\mathtt{R}_{U}(T)<\frac{T}{\log(NT)}\,.

Step 2

If
T>16
, then
\frac{NT}{\log(NT)}>N\log T
.

If
T>\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{N}\log^{2}(N\texttt{q}_{I})
,
then

\displaystyle\sqrt{NT}

\displaystyle>\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}(\log\texttt{q}_{I})\log^{2}(N\texttt{q}_{I})}>\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})}\,\sqrt{(\log\texttt{q}_{I})\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})}

\displaystyle>\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I})}\,\log(\texttt{q}_{I}\log(N\texttt{q}_{I}))>\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}/3}\log(\texttt{q}_{I}\mathsf{r}_{I}/3)

and hence

\frac{NT}{\log(NT)}>c\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}T\mathsf{r}_{I}N}

So if
T>\max\Big\{16,\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\log\texttt{q}_{I}}{N}\log^{2}(N\texttt{q}_{I})\Big\}
, then
\frac{NT}{\log(NT)}>cN\mathtt{R}_{I}(T)
.

G.2
Lower Bound on Cold Start

In this section, we prove the lower bound on the cold start time. Using the definition of cold start time in Eq. (
2
) we will show that there exists
\gamma^{\prime}>0
such that for all
T
such that

\displaystyle T<\min\Big\{\log N\,\log\texttt{q}_{U},\max\big\{\frac{\texttt{q}_{I}\log^{2}\texttt{q}_{I}}{N},16\big\}\Big\}

(110)

then

\displaystyle\mathtt{regret}(T)>\gamma^{\prime}\frac{T}{\log(NT)}

(111)

Note that according to Theorem
4.12
,

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq c\max\Big\{

\displaystyle N,\min\{NT,N\log\texttt{q}_{U},\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\},\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\Big\}\Big\}\,.

We use this to show that for all
T
lower bounded as in (
110
), the lower bound in Eq. (
111
) holds.

We will also use the assumptions in Figure
2
.
Additionally, we will use use the assumption
\texttt{q}_{U}>(\log\texttt{q}_{I})^{2}
and
\texttt{q}_{I}>(\log N)^{5}
and definitions of
\mathsf{s}_{U}
and
\mathsf{s}_{I}
to get
c\log\texttt{q}_{U}<\mathsf{s}_{U}\leq C\log\texttt{q}_{U}
and and
c\log\texttt{q}_{I}<\mathsf{r}_{I}<C\log\texttt{q}_{I}
.
For the sake of simplicity, we will ignore all universal constant factors denoting them by
c
12
12

12

Throughout, we will use the property that if
x>2A\log A
then
x/\log x>A
and if
x<A\log A
then
x/\log x<A
.
.

We look at different regimes for
T
seprately:

Step 1

If
T<16
then

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq cN>c^{\prime}\frac{NT}{\log(NT)}\,.

Step 2

If
T<\min\{\log\texttt{q}_{U},\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}/N\}
for a constant
\Gamma
, we have

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq c\min\{NT,N\log\texttt{q}_{U},\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\}\geq cNT>c\frac{NT}{\log(NT)}\,.

Step 3

If
\log\texttt{q}_{U}\leq T<\min\{(\log N)(\log\texttt{q}_{U}),\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}/N\}
we have

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq c\min\{NT,N\log\texttt{q}_{U},\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}\}\geq cN\log\texttt{q}_{U}=cN\log\texttt{q}_{U}\frac{\log N}{\log N}>c\frac{NT}{\log(NT)}\,.

Step 4

If
\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}/N\leq T<\min\{(\log N)(\log\texttt{q}_{U}),(\texttt{q}_{I}/N)\log^{2}\texttt{q}_{I}\}
we have

\displaystyle N\mathtt{regret}(T)\geq c\min\Big\{\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}},\sqrt{T\texttt{q}_{I}N},N\log\texttt{q}_{U}\Big\}\geq c\frac{NT}{\log\texttt{q}_{I}}>c\frac{NT}{\log(NT)}\,.

where in the last inequality we used
NT\geq\sqrt{\texttt{q}_{I}}
hence
\log(NT)\geq c\log\texttt{q}_{I}
.

Appendix H
Concentration Lemmas

H.1
Tail Bounds

The following lemma is derived by application of Chernoff bound to Binomial variables
[
18
]
.

Lemma H.1

(Chernoff bound)
.

Let
X_{1},\cdots,X_{n}\in[0,1]
be independent random variables. Let
X=\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}
and
\bar{X}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}
. Then, for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[X\geq(1+\epsilon)\bar{X}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2+\epsilon}\bar{X}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{\exp\Big(-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}\bar{X}\Big),\exp\Big(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\bar{X}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[X\leq(1-\epsilon)\bar{X}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\bar{X}\Big)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[|X-\bar{X}|\geq\epsilon\bar{X}\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\max\Big\{\exp\Big(-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}\bar{X}\Big),\exp\Big(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\bar{X}\Big)\Big\}

Lemma H.2

(McDiarmid
[
32
]
)
.

Let
X_{1},\cdots,X_{n}
be a martingale adapted to filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{n})
satisfying

(i)

\mathrm{Var}(X_{i}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1})\leq\sigma_{i}^{2}
, for
1\leq i\leq n
, and

(ii)

|X_{i}-X_{i-1}|\leq M
, for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Let
X=\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}
.
Then

\mathbb{P}\big[X-\mathbb{E}X\geq r\big]\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{r^{2}}{2\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}^{2}+Mr/3\big)}\bigg)\,.

H.2
Lemmas on Balls and Bins Probelm

Lemma H.3

(Balls and bins: tail bound for number of nonempty bins)
.

Suppose
m\leq\frac{4}{15}n
. If
m
balls are placed into
n
bins each independently and uniformly at random, then with probability at least
1-\exp(-m/2)
at least
m/2
bins are nonempty.

Proof.

Let random variable
E
be the number of empty bins and
n-E
is the number of nonempty bins. We want to show that
\mathbb{P}[n-E\geq m/2]>1-\exp(-m/2)
.

Any configuration with at most
m/2
nonempty bins has at least
n-m/2
empty bins. Thus to upper bound
\mathbb{P}[E>n-m/2]
we may bound the probability of having
some
set of
n-m/2
bins be empty.
There are
{n\choose n-m/2}={n\choose m/2}
possible choices for these empty bins, and each ball has to land outside of these, which has probability
[(m/2)/n]^{m}
. Thus, the probability of at most
m/2
nonempty bins is bounded by

{n\choose m/2}\left(\frac{m/2}{n}\right)^{m}\leq\left(\frac{n\cdot e}{m/2}\right)^{m/2}\left(\frac{m/2}{n}\right)^{m}\leq\left(\frac{me}{2n}\right)^{m/2}\leq\exp(-m/2)\,,

where we used
m\leq\frac{4}{15}n
in the last inequality.
∎

Lemma H.4

(Balls and bins: tail bound for number of nonempty bins)
.

Suppose
m,n\geq k
. If
m
balls are placed into
n
bins each independently and uniformly at random, then with probability at least
1-\exp(-k/3)
at least
k/3
bins are nonempty.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of Lemma
H.4
.

Any configuration with at most
k/3
nonempty bins has at least
n-k/3
empty bins.
There are
{n\choose n-k/3}={n\choose k/3}
possible choices for these empty bins, and each ball has to land outside of these, which has probability
[(k/3)/n]^{m}
. Thus, the probability of at most
k/3
nonempty bins is bounded by

{n\choose k/3}\left(\frac{k/3}{n}\right)^{m}\leq\left(\frac{n\cdot e}{k/3}\right)^{k/3}\left(\frac{k/3}{n}\right)^{m}\leq\left(\frac{n\cdot e}{k/3}\right)^{k/3}\left(\frac{k/3}{n}\right)^{k}\leq\left(\frac{e}{9}\right)^{k/3}\leq\exp(-k/3)\,,

where we used
m,n\geq k
.
∎

Lemma H.5

(Balls and bins: tail bound for number of nonempty bins)
.

Suppose
m\geq n\log(n/\delta)
. If
m
balls are placed into
n
bins each independently and uniformly at random, then with probability at least
1-\delta
all bins are nonempty.

Proof.

The probability of having at least on empty bin is at most
n(1-1/n)^{m}\leq n\exp(-m/n)
.
∎

The following lemma records a simple consequence of linearity of expectation.

Lemma H.6

(Balls and bins: bound for the expected number of nonempty bins)
.

If we throw
m
balls into
n
bins independently uniformly at random, then, the expected number of nonempty bins is
n(1-(1-1/n)^{m})\,.

Appendix I
Converting to Anytime Regret

The
doubling trick
converts
an online algorithm designed for a finite known time horizon to an algorithm that does not require knowledge of the time horizon and yet achieves the same regret (up to multiplicative constant) at any time
[
17
,
29
]
(i.e.,
anytime regret
).

The trick is to divide time into intervals and restart algorithm at the beginning of each interval.
Let
A(T)
be an online algorithm taking the known time horizon as input and achieving regret
\mathrm{R}(T)
at time
T
.
There are two regret scalings of interest. (1) If
\mathrm{R}(T)=O(T^{\alpha})
for some
0<\alpha<1
, then to achieve anytime regret, the doubling trick uses time intervals of length
2,2^{2},2^{3},..,2^{m}
. This achieves regret of at most
\mathrm{R}(T)/(1-2^{\alpha})
at time
T
for any
T
. (2) Alternatively, if
\mathrm{R}(T)=O(\log T)
, then using intervals of length
2^{2},2^{2^{2}},..,2^{2^{m}}
achieves regret of at most
4\mathrm{R}(T)
at time
T
for any
T
.

Clearly, different scalings can be used before and after some threshold
\mathsf{\tau}
if the algorithm achieves regret
O(\log T)
for
T<\mathsf{\tau}
and
O(\sqrt{T})
if
T\geq\mathsf{\tau}
, as is the case for the proposed algorithm.
